edge of the clock with undefined order\n139:     // vs all other always_ff triggered on the positive edge of the clock. +---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n107: \n108: See the :ref:`performance-counters` documentation for a description of the counter registers.\n109: \n110: \n111: Machine Status (mstatus)\n112: ------------------------\n113: \n114: CSR Address: ``0x300``\n115: \n116: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0080``\n117: \n118: +-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n119: | Bit#  | R/W | Description                                                                     |\n120: +-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n121: | 21    | RW  | **TW:** Timeout Wait (WFI executed in User Mode will trap to Machine Mode).     |\n122: +-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n123: | 17    | RW  | **MPRV:** Modify Privilege (Loads and stores use MPP for privilege checking).   |\n124: +-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n125: | 12:11 | RW  | **MPP:** Machine Previous Privilege mode.                                       |\n126: +-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n127: | 7     | RW  | **Previous Interrupt Enable (MPIE)**, i.e., before entering exception handling. |\n128: +-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n129: | 3     | RW  | **Interrupt Enable (MIE):** If set to 1'b1, interrupts are globally enabled.    |\n130: +-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n131: \n132: When an exception is encountered, ``mstatus``.MPIE will be set to ``mstatus``.MIE, and ``mstatus``.MPP will be set to the current privilege mode.\n133: When the MRET instruction is executed, the value of MPIE will be stored back to ``mstatus``.MIE, and the privilege mode will be restored from ``mstatus``.MPP.\n134: \n135: If you want to enable interrupt handling in your exception handler, set ``mstatus``.MIE to 1'b1 inside your handler code.\n136: \n137: Only Machine Mode and User Mode are supported.\n138: Any write to ``mstatus``.MPP of an unsupported value will be interpreted as Machine Mode.\n139: \n140: Machine ISA Register (misa)\n141: ---------------------------\n142: \n143: CSR Address: ``0x301``\n144: \n145: ``misa`` is a WARL register which describes the ISA supported by the hart.\n146: On Ibex, ``misa`` is hard-wired, i.e. it will remain unchanged after any write.\n147: \n148: \n149: Machine Interrupt Enable Register (mie)\n150: ---------------------------------------\n151: \n152: CSR Address: ``0x304``\n153: \n154: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n155: \n156: ``mie`` is a WARL register which allows to individually enable/disable local interrupts.\n157: After reset, all interrupts are disabled.\n158: \n159: +-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n160: | Bit#  | Interrupt                                                                            |\n161: +-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n162: | 30:16 | Machine Fast Interrupt Enables: Set bit x+16 to enable                               |\n163: |       | fast interrupt ``irq_fast_i[x]``.                                                    |\n164: +-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n165: | 11    | **Machine External Interrupt Enable (MEIE):** If set, ``irq_external_i`` is enabled. |\n166: +-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n167: | 7     | **Machine Timer Interrupt Enable (MTIE):** If set, ``irq_timer_i`` is enabled.       |\n168: +-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n169: | 3     | **Machine Software Interrupt Enable (MSIE):** if set, ``irq_software_i`` is enabled. |\n170: +-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n171: \n172: \n173: Machine Trap-Vector Base Address (mtvec)\n174: ----------------------------------------\n175: \n176: CSR Address: ``0x305``\n177: \n178: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0001``\n179: \n180: ``mtvec`` is a WARL register which contains the machine trap-vector base address.\n181: \n182: +-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n183: | Bit#  | Interrupt                                                                            |\n184: +-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n185: | 31:2  | **BASE:** The trap-vector base address, always aligned to 256 bytes, i.e.,           |\n186: |       | ``mtvec[7:2]`` is always set to 6'b0.                                                |\n187: +-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n188: | 1:0   | **MODE:** Always set to 2'b01 to indicate vectored interrupt handling (read-only).   |\n189: +-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n190: \n191: \n192: Machine Exception PC (mepc)\n193: ---------------------------\n194: \n195: CSR Address: ``0x341``\n196: \n197: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n198: \n199: When an exception is encountered, the current program counter is saved in ``mepc``, and the core jumps to the exception address.\n200: When an MRET instruction is executed, the value from ``mepc`` replaces the current program counter.\n201: \n202: \n203: Machine Cause (mcause)\n204: ----------------------\n205: \n206: CSR Address: ``0x342``\n207: \n208: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n209: \n210: +-------+-----+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n211: | Bit#  | R/W | Description                                                      |\n212: +-------+-----+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n213: | 31    | R   | **Interrupt:** This bit is set when the exception was triggered  |\n214: |       |     | by an interrupt.                                                 |\n215: +-------+-----+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n216: | 4:0   | R   | **Exception Code**                                               |\n217: +-------+-----+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n218: \n219: When an exception is encountered, the corresponding exception code is stored in this register.\n220: \n221: \n222: Machine Trap Value (mtval)\n223: --------------------------\n224: \n225: CSR Address: ``0x343``\n226: \n227: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n228: \n229: When an exception is encountered, this register can hold exception-specific information to assist software in handling the trap.\n230: \n231:  * In the case of errors in the load-store unit ``mtval`` holds the address of the transaction causing the error.\n232:  * If this transaction is misaligned, ``mtval`` holds the address of the missing transaction part.\n233:  * In the case of illegal instruction exceptions, ``mtval`` holds the actual faulting instruction.\n234: \n235: For all other exceptions, ``mtval`` is 0.\n236: \n237: \n238: Machine Interrupt Pending Register (mip)\n239: ----------------------------------------\n240: \n241: CSR Address: ``0x344``\n242: \n243: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n244: \n245: ``mip`` is a read-only register indicating pending interrupt requests.\n246: A particular bit in the register reads as one if the corresponding interrupt input signal is high and if the interrupt is enabled in the ``mie`` CSR.\n247: \n248: +-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n249: | Bit#  | Interrupt                                                                             |\n250: +-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n251: | 30:16 | Machine Fast Interrupts Pending: If bit x+16 is set,                                  |\n252: |       | fast interrupt ``irq_fast_i[x]`` is pending.                                          |\n253: +-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n254: | 11    | **Machine External Interrupt Pending (MEIP):** If set, ``irq_external_i`` is pending. |\n255: +-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n256: | 7     | **Machine Timer Interrupt Pending (MTIP):** If set, ``irq_timer_i`` is pending.       |\n257: +-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n258: | 3     | **Machine Software Interrupt Pending (MSIP):** if set, ``irq_software_i`` is pending. |\n259: +-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n260: \n261: PMP Configuration Register (pmpcfgx)\n262: ------------------------------------\n263: \n264: CSR Address: ``0x3A0 - 0x3A3``\n265: \n266: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n267: \n268: ``pmpcfgx`` are registers to configure PMP regions. Each register configures 4 PMP regions.\n269: \n270: +---------+---------+---------+---------+\n271: |  31:24  |  23:16  |  15:8   |   7:0   |\n272: +---------+---------+---------+---------+\n273: | pmp3cfg | pmp2cfg | pmp1cfg | pmp0cfg |\n274: +---------+---------+---------+---------+\n275: \n276: The configuration fields for each region are as follows:\n277: \n278: +-------+--------------------------+\n279: | Bit#  |  Definition              |\n280: +-------+--------------------------+\n281: |    7  | Lock                     |\n282: +-------+--------------------------+\n283: |  6:5  | Reserved (Read as zero)  |\n284: +-------+--------------------------+\n285: |  4:3  | Mode                     |\n286: +-------+--------------------------+\n287: |    2  | Execute permission       |\n288: +-------+--------------------------+\n289: |    1  | Write permission         |\n290: +-------+--------------------------+\n291: |    0  | Read permission          |\n292: +-------+--------------------------+\n293: \n294: Details of these configuration bits can be found in the RISC-V Privileged Specification, version 1.11 (see Physical Memory Protection CSRs, Section 3.6.1).\n295: \n296: Note that the combination of Write permission = 1, Read permission = 0 is reserved, and will be treated by the core as Read/Write permission = 0.\n297: \n298: PMP Address Register (pmpaddrx)\n299: -------------------------------\n300: \n301: CSR Address: ``0x3B0 - 0x3BF``\n302: \n303: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n304: \n305: ``pmpaddrx`` are registers to set address matching for PMP regions.\n306: \n307: +----------------+\n308: |     31:0       |\n309: +----------------+\n310: | address[33:2]  |\n311: +----------------+\n312: \n313: Machine Security Configuration (mseccfg/mseccfgh)\n314: -------------------------------------------------\n315: \n316: CSR Address: ``mseccfg``: ``0x747``  ``mseccfg``: ``0x757``\n317: \n318: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000_0000_0000``\n319: \n320: +------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n321: | Bit# | Definition                                                                                                                        |\n322: +------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n323: | 2    | **Rule Locking Bypass (RLB):** If set locked PMP entries can be modified                                                          |\n324: +------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n325: | 1    | **Machine Mode Whitelist Policy (MMWP):** If set default policy for PMP is deny for M-Mode accesses that don't match a PMP region |\n326: +------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n327: | 0    | **Machine Mode Lockdown (MML):** Alters behaviour of ``pmpcfgX`` bits                                                             |\n328: +------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n329: \n330: ``mseccfg`` is specified in the Trusted Execution Environment (TEE) working group proposal `PMP Enhancements for memory access and execution prevention on Machine mode (Smepmp) version 0.9.3 <https://github.com/riscv/riscv-tee/blob/61455747230a26002d741f64879dd78cc9689323/Smepmp/Smepmp.pdf>`_, which gives the full details of it's functionality including the new PMP behaviour when ``mseccfg.MML`` is set.\n331: Note that the reset value means PMP behavior out of reset matches the RISC-V Privileged Architecture.\n332: A write to ``mseccfg`` is required to change it.\n333: Note ``mseccfgh`` reads as all 0s and ignores all writes.\n334: Any access to ``mseccfg`` or ``mseccfgh`` when using an Ibex configuration without PMP (``PMPEnable`` is 0) will trigger an illegal instruction exception.\n335: \n336: .. _csr-tselect:\n337: \n338: Trigger Select Register (tselect)\n339: ---------------------------------\n340: \n341: CSR Address: ``0x7A0``\n342: \n343: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n344: \n345: Accessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n346: \n347: Number of the currently selected trigger starting at 0.\n348: The number of triggers is configured by the DbgHwNumLen parameter.\n349: \n350: Writing a value larger than or equal to the number of supported triggers will write the highest valid index.\n351: This allows a debugger to detect the allowed number of triggers by reading back the value.\n352: \n353: .. _csr-tdata1:\n354: \n355: Trigger Data Register 1 (tdata1)\n356: --------------------------------\n357: \n358: CSR Address: ``0x7A1``\n359: \n360: Reset Value: ``0x2800_1000``\n361: \n362: Accessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n363: Since native triggers are not supported, writes to this register from M-Mode will be ignored.\n364: \n365: Ibex only implements one type of trigger, instruction address match.\n366: Most fields of this register will read as a fixed value to reflect the mode that is supported.\n367: \n368: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n369: | Bit#  | R/W  | Description                                                      |\n370: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n371: | 31:28 | R    | **type:** 2 = Address/Data match trigger type.                   |\n372: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n373: | 27    | R    | **dmode:** 1 = Only debug mode can write tdata registers         |\n374: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n375: | 26:21 | R    | **maskmax:** 0 = Only exact matching supported.                  |\n376: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n377: | 20    | R    | **hit:** 0 = Hit indication not supported.                       |\n378: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n379: | 19    | R    | **select:** 0 = Only address matching is supported.              |\n380: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n381: | 18    | R    | **timing:** 0 = Break before the instruction at the specified    |\n382: |       |      | address.                                                         |\n383: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n384: | 17:16 | R    | **sizelo:** 0 = Match accesses of any size.                      |\n385: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n386: | 15:12 | R    | **action:** 1 = Enter debug mode on match.                       |\n387: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n388: | 11    | R    | **chain:** 0 = Chaining not supported.                           |\n389: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n390: | 10:7  | R    | **match:** 0 = Match the whole address.                          |\n391: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n392: | 6     | R    | **m:** 1 = Match in M-Mode.                                      |\n393: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n394: | 5     | R    | zero.                                                            |\n395: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n396: | 4     | R    | **s:** 0 = S-Mode not supported.                                 |\n397: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n398: | 3     | R    | **u:** 1 = Match in U-Mode.                                      |\n399: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n400: | 2     | RW   | **execute:** Enable matching on instruction address.             |\n401: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n402: | 1     | R    | **store:** 0 = Store address / data matching not supported.      |\n403: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n404: | 0     | R    | **load:** 0 = Load address / data matching not supported.        |\n405: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n406: \n407: Details of these configuration bits can be found in the RISC-V Debug Specification, version 0.13.2 (see Trigger Registers, Section 5.2).\n408: \n409: .. _csr-tdata2:\n410: \n411: Trigger Data Register 2 (tdata2)\n412: --------------------------------\n413: \n414: CSR Address: ``0x7A2``\n415: \n416: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n417: \n418: Accessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n419: Since native triggers are not supported, writes to this register from M-Mode will be ignored.\n420: \n421: This register stores the instruction address to match against for a breakpoint trigger.\n422: \n423: Trigger Data Register 3 (tdata3)\n424: --------------------------------\n425: \n426: CSR Address: ``0x7A3``\n427: \n428: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n429: \n430: Accessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n431: \n432: Ibex does not support the features requiring this register, so writes are ignored and it will always read as zero.\n433: \n434: Machine Context Register (mcontext)\n435: -----------------------------------\n436: \n437: CSR Address: ``0x7A8``\n438: \n439: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n440: \n441: Accessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n442: \n443: Ibex does not support the features requiring this register, so writes are ignored and it will always read as zero.\n444: \n445: Supervisor Context Register (scontext)\n446: --------------------------------------\n447: \n448: CSR Address: ``0x7AA``\n449: \n450: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n451: \n452: Accessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n453: \n454: Ibex does not support the features requiring this register, so writes are ignored and it will always read as zero.\n455: \n456: .. _csr-dcsr:\n457: \n458: Debug Control and Status Register (dcsr)\n459: ----------------------------------------\n460: \n461: CSR Address: ``0x7B0``\n462: \n463: Reset Value: ``0x4000_0003``\n464: \n465: Accessible in Debug Mode only.\n466: Ibex implements the following bit fields.\n467: Other bit fields read as zero.\n468: \n469: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n470: | Bit#  | R/W  | Description                                                      |\n471: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n472: | 31:28 | R    | **xdebugver:** 4 = External spec-compliant debug support exists. |\n473: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n474: | 15    | RW   | **ebreakm:** EBREAK in M-Mode behaves as described in Privileged |\n475: |       |      | Spec (0), or enters Debug Mode (1).                              |\n476: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n477: | 12    | WARL | **ebreaku:** EBREAK in U-Mode behaves as described in Privileged |\n478: |       |      | Spec (0), or enters Debug Mode (1).                              |\n479: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n480: | 8:6   | R    | **cause:** 1 = EBREAK, 2 = trigger, 3 = halt request, 4 = step   |\n481: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n482: | 2     | RW   | **step:** When set and not in Debug Mode, execute a single       |\n483: |       |      | instruction and enter Debug Mode.                                |\n484: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n485: | 1:0   | WARL | **prv:** Privilege level the core was operating in when Debug    |\n486: |       |      | Mode was entered. May be modified by debugger to change          |\n487: |       |      | privilege level. Ibex allows transitions to all supported modes. |\n488: |       |      | (M- and U-Mode).                                                 |\n489: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n490: \n491: Details of these configuration bits can be found in the RISC-V Debug Specification, version 0.13.2 (see Core Debug Registers, Section 4.8).\n492: Note that **ebreaku** and **prv** are accidentally specified as RW in version 0.13.2 of the RISC-V Debug Specification.\n493: More recent versions of the specification define these fields correctly as WARL.\n494: \n495: .. _csr-dpc:\n496: \n497: Debug PC Register (dpc)\n498: -----------------------\n499: \n500: CSR Address: ``0x7B1``\n501: \n502: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n503: \n504: When entering Debug Mode, ``dpc`` is updated with the address of the next instruction that would be executed (if Debug Mode would not have been entered).\n505: When resuming, the PC is set to the address stored in ``dpc``.\n506: The debug module may modify ``dpc``.\n507: Accessible in Debug Mode only.\n508: \n509: Debug Scratch Register 0 (dscratch0)\n510: ------------------------------------\n511: \n512: CSR Address: ``0x7B2``\n513: \n514: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n515: \n516: Scratch register to be used by the debug module.\n517: Accessible in Debug Mode only.\n518: \n519: Debug Scratch Register 1 (dscratch1)\n520: ------------------------------------\n521: \n522: CSR Address: ``0x7B3``\n523: \n524: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n525: \n526: Scratch register to be used by the debug module.\n527: Accessible in Debug Mode only.\n528: \n529: CPU Control and Status Register (cpuctrlsts)\n530: --------------------------------------------\n531: \n532: CSR Address: ``0x7C0``\n533: \n534: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n535: \n536: Custom CSR to control runtime configuration of CPU components.\n537: Accessible in Machine Mode only.\n538: Ibex implements the following bit fields.\n539: Other bit fields read as zero.\n540: \n541: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n542: | Bit#  | R/W  | Description                                                      |\n543: +=======+======+==================================================================+\n544: | 8     | R    | **ic_scr_key_valid:** The icache scrambling key is valid. A      |\n545: |       |      | ``fence.i`` instruction is guaranteed to fetch a new key. If     |\n546: |       |      | the instruction cache has not been configured or the core has    |\n547: |       |      | not been configured with security features  (ICache parameter    |\n548: |       |      | == 0 or SecureIbex parameter == 0), this field will always read  |\n549: |       |      | as zero. (see :ref:`icache-scramble-key`)                        |\n550: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n551: | 7     | RW   | **double_fault_seen:** A synchronous exception was observed when |\n552: |       |      | the ``sync_exc_seen`` field was set. This field must be manually |\n553: |       |      | cleared, hardware only sets it (see :ref:`double-fault-detect`). |\n554: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n555: | 6     | RW   | **sync_exc_seen:** A synchronous exception has been observed.    |\n556: |       |      | This flag is cleared when ``mret`` is executed.                  |\n557: |       |      | (see :ref:`double-fault-detect`).                                |\n558: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n559: | 5:3   | WARL | **dummy_instr_mask:** Mask to control frequency of dummy         |\n560: |       |      | instruction insertion. If the core has not been configured with  |\n561: |       |      | security features (SecureIbex parameter == 0), this field will   |\n562: |       |      | always read as zero (see :ref:`security`).                       |\n563: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n564: | 2     | WARL | **dummy_instr_en:** Enable (1) or disable (0) dummy instruction  |\n565: |       |      | insertion features. If the core has not been configured with     |\n566: |       |      | security features (SecureIbex parameter == 0), this field will   |\n567: |       |      | always read as zero (see :ref:`security`).                       |\n568: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n569: | 1     | WARL | **data_ind_timing:** Enable (1) or disable (0) data-independent  |\n570: |       |      | timing features. If the core has not been configured with        |\n571: |       |      | security features (SecureIbex parameter == 0), this field will   |\n572: |       |      | always read as zero.                                             |\n573: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n574: | 0     | WARL | **icache_enable:** Enable (1) or disable (0) the instruction     |\n575: |       |      | cache. If the instruction cache has not been configured (ICache  |\n576: |       |      | parameter == 0), this field will always read as zero.            |\n577: +-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n578: \n579: Security Feature Seed Register (secureseed)\n580: -------------------------------------------\n581: \n582: CSR Address: ``0x7C1``\n583: \n584: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n585: \n586: Accessible in Machine Mode only.\n587: \n588: Custom CSR to allow re-seeding of security-related pseudo-random number generators.\n589: A write to this register will update the seeding of pseudo-random number generators inside the design.\n590: This allows software to improve the randomness, and therefore security, of certain features by periodically reading from a true random number generator peripheral.\n591: Seed values are not actually stored in a register and so reads to this register will always return zero.\n592: \n593: Time Registers (time(h))\n594: ------------------------\n595: \n596: CSR Address: ``0xC01 / 0xC81``\n597: \n598: The User Mode ``time(h)`` registers are not implemented in Ibex.\n599: Any access to these registers will trap.\n600: It is recommended that trap handler software provides a means of accessing platform-defined ``mtime(h)`` timers where available.\n601: \n602: Machine Vendor ID (mvendorid)\n603: -----------------------------\n604: \n605: CSR Address: ``0xF11``\n606: \n607: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n608: \n609: Use the top-level parameter ``CsrMvendorId`` in :file:`rtl/ibex_top.sv` to change the fixed value.\n610: Details of what the ID represents can be found in the RISC-V Privileged Specification.\n611: \n612: Machine Architecture ID (marchid)\n613: ---------------------------------\n614: \n615: CSR Address: ``0xF12``\n616: \n617: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0016``\n618: \n619: Use the ``CSR_MARCHID_VALUE`` parameter in :file:`rtl/ibex_pkg.sv` to change the fixed value.\n620: The value used is allocated specifically to Ibex.\n621: If significant changes are made a different ID should be used.\n622: Details of what the ID represents can be found in the RISC-V Privileged Specification.\n623: \n624: Machine Implementation ID (mimpid)\n625: ----------------------------------\n626: \n627: CSR Address: ``0xF13``\n628: \n629: Reset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n630: \n631: Use the top-level parameter ``CsrMimpId`` in :file:`rtl/ibex_top.sv` to change the fixed value.\n632: Details of what the ID represents can be found in the RISC-V Privileged Specification.\n633: \n634: .. _csr-mhartid:\n635: \n636: Hardware Thread ID (mhartid)\n637: ----------------------------\n638: \n639: CSR Address: ``0xF14``\n640: \n641: Reads directly return the value of the ``hart_id_i`` input signal.\n642: See also :ref:`core-integration`.\n\n(End of file - total 642 See :ref:`instruction-fetch` for details.\n15: \n16: Instruction Decode and Execute (ID/EX)\n17:   Decodes fetched instruction and immediately executes it, register read and write all occurs in this stage.\n18:   Multi-cycle instructions will stall this stage until they are complete See :ref:`instruction-decode-execute` for details.\n19: \n20: All instructions require two cycles minimum to pass down the pipeline.\n21: One cycle in the IF stage and one in the ID/EX stage.\n22: Not all instructions can complete in the ID/EX stage in one cycle so will stall there until they complete.\n23: This means the maximum IPC (Instructions per Cycle) Ibex can achieve is 1 when multi-cycle instructions aren't used.\n24: See Multi- and Single-Cycle Instructions below for the details.\n25: \n26: Third Pipeline Stage\n27: --------------------\n28: Ibex can be configured to have a third pipeline stage (Writeback) which has major effects on performance and instruction behaviour.\n29: The details of its impact are not yet documented here.\n30: All of the information presented below applies only to the two stage pipeline provided in the default configurations.\n31: \n32: Multi- and Single-Cycle Instructions\n33: ------------------------------------\n34: \n35: In the table below when an instruction stalls for X cycles X + 1 cycles pass before a new instruction enters the ID/EX stage.\n36: Some instructions stall for a variable time, this is indicated as a range e.g. 1 - \n872:   if (SecureIbex) begin : g_check_mem_response\n873:     // For secure configurations only process load/store responses if we're expecting them to guard\n874:     // against false responses being injected on to the bus\n875:     assign lsu_load_err  = lsu_load_err_raw  & (outstanding_load_wb  | expecting_load_resp_id);\n876:     assign lsu_store_err = lsu_store_err_raw & g_no_check_mem_response\n879:     // For non-secure configurations trust the bus protocol is being followed and we'll only ever\n880:     // see a response if we have an outstanding request.\n881:     assign lsu_load_err  = lsu_load_err_raw;\n882:     assign lsu_store_err = lsu_store_err_raw;\n883:     assign rf_we_lsu     = lsu_rdata_valid;\n884: \n885:     // unused_expecting_load_resp_id;\n888:     logic unused_expecting_store_resp_id;\n889: \n890:     assign unused_expecting_load_resp_id  = expecting_load_resp_id;\n891:     assign
